## Notes

- `pipecat`: packages/core/src/index.ts: version() plus the import-time version banner, which is skipped for the pipecat/pc CLI (checks process.argv[1]). The version is hardcoded to the core package version (0.0.0); upstream reads the pipecat-ai distribution metadata. Not ported, because they are Python packaging specific: the check for a separately installed pipecat-ai-flows, and the wait_for2 patch for Python < 3.12. The banner is an import side effect, while package.json says sideEffects:false. | UNRESOLVED: audio/resamplers/soxr_resampler.ts and soxr_stream_resampler.ts: soxrResample and SoxrResampleStream are stubs that throw. So createFileResampler/createStreamResampler, ulawToPcm/pcmToUlaw/alawToPcm/pcmToAlaw at different rates, loadDtmfAudio at any rate other than 8000, and LocalSmartTurnAnalyzerV3 at any rate other than 16 kHz all throw at runtime. This needs an injected libsoxr WASM build or a pure-TS resampler fallback in core, which is a design decision beyond a parity fix. | UNRESOLVED: audio/krisp_instance.ts: the krisp_audio SDK is a stub until setKrispAudioSdk() is called. LocalSmartTurnAnalyzerV3 needs an injected createSession (an ONNX runtime), and the PIPECAT_SMART_TURN_LOG_DATA WAV dump is a stub that only logs a warning because core has no filesystem. | UNRESOLVED: adapters/schemas/direct_function.ts: upstream sets each parameter's 'description' from the docstring. The TS metadata (DirectFunctionMetadata) has no per-parameter description field, so descriptions only appear if they are put inside the JSON-schema hint. Also, DirectFunctionWrapper.invoke silently drops extra argument names, where Python's function(params=..., **args) raises TypeError. Fixing either would mean inventing API, so I left both. | UNRESOLVED: audio/turn/smart_turn/base_smart_turn.ts: an edge case remains. If sampleRate is 0, maxSamples is 0; Python's segment_audio[-0:] keeps the whole array, while the TS slice gives an empty one. It only happens if setSampleRate was never called, so I left it. | UNRESOLVED: audio/utils.ts: the reimplemented ITU-R BS.1770 integratedLoudness (replacing the Python 'loudness' package) and the float32 accumulation in the whisper-feature normalization could not be checked numerically against upstream here. No tests exist for these modules.
- `pipecat.adapters`: Upstream __init__.py is empty, so index.ts only has the header and `export {}`. | UNRESOLVED: packages/core/src/bus/serializers/json.ts imports `@pipedog/bus-adapters/index` (LLMContextAdapter, ToolsSchemaAdapter), and that import does not resolve. @pipedog/core's package.json does not declare bus-adapters, bus-base, bus-llm-context-adapter or bus-tools-schema-adapter, and core's node_modules links only asyncio. Running the module fails with "Cannot find module '@pipedog/bus-adapters/index'". Those packages also depend on @pipedog/core, so declaring them would create a dependency cycle and break the core-takes-no-deps rule. The likely fix is to keep bus/adapters (base, llm_context_adapter, tools_schema_adapter) in core, but that is a manifest/packaging decision I was told not to make. I checked it with temporary symlinks, which I then removed: with the packages linked, serialize/deserialize round-trips and tsc reports no errors in the reviewed files. | UNRESOLVED: Minor, left as is: Python's json.dumps escapes non-ASCII and JSON.stringify does not, so the wire bytes differ but parse to the same values. JSONMessageSerializer serializes a dataclass from Object.entries, not dataclasses.fields, and passes every decoded field to the constructor instead of splitting init and non-init fields. str()/repr() of None, floats and bools in classifier error messages and in OpenAI Decisions probability keys does not match Python exactly (for example 'None' vs '', 1.0 vs 1).
- `pipecat.adapters.base_llm_adapter`: Dropped as deprecated: the __init_subclass__ warning for a sync get_llm_invocation_params. Kept: _warnContextSystemMessage, which still handles deprecated context system messages, now as a logger.warning instead of warn_deprecated. builtinTools is a Record<string, FunctionSchema>, not a Map, to match how services/llm_service.ts uses it. warnings.warn(UserWarning) became logger.warning. urlsplit().scheme became a urlScheme() helper. The broad catch in prepareFileContent calls rethrowIfCancelled. getLlmInvocationParams takes (context, options?: object). | UNRESOLVED: audio/resamplers/soxr_resampler.ts and soxr_stream_resampler.ts: soxrResample and SoxrResampleStream are stubs that throw. So createFileResampler/createStreamResampler, ulawToPcm/pcmToUlaw/alawToPcm/pcmToAlaw at different rates, loadDtmfAudio at any rate other than 8000, and LocalSmartTurnAnalyzerV3 at any rate other than 16 kHz all throw at runtime. - `pipecat.adapters.schemas.direct_function`: DIVERGENCE: JS has no runtime type hints or docstrings. Parameter names and required-ness (no default value) are parsed from the function source. The description and per-parameter types come from optional metadata on the function (DirectFunctionMetadata: description, and parameters as JSON schemas, JS constructors, null, or unions given as arrays). Parameters with no metadata get {}. Minified functions lose their parameter names. invoke(args, params) passes args by matching names to the parsed parameters. The async check uses constructor.name === 'AsyncFunction'. tool_options became toolOptions(fn, opts?) or toolOptions(opts)(fn), which sets _pipecatCancelOnInterruption, _pipecatTimeoutSecs and _pipecatCancellableByLlm. llm_service.ts reads these same names. | UNRESOLVED: audio/resamplers/soxr_resampler.ts and soxr_stream_resampler.ts: soxrResample and SoxrResampleStream are stubs that throw. - `pipecat.audio`: Empty upstream __init__.py; index.ts is `export {}`. | UNRESOLVED: packages/core/src/bus/serializers/json.ts imports `@pipedog/bus-adapters/index` (LLMContextAdapter, ToolsSchemaAdapter), and that import does not resolve. @pipedog/core's package.json does not declare bus-adapters, bus-base, bus-llm-context-adapter or bus-tools-schema-adapter, and core's node_modules links only asyncio. Running the module fails with "Cannot find module '@pipedog/bus-adapters/index'". Those packages also depend on @pipedog/core, so declaring them would create a dependency cycle and break the core-takes-no-deps rule. The likely fix is to keep bus/adapters (base, llm_context_adapter, tools_schema_adapter) in core, but that is a manifest/packaging decision I was told not to make. - `pipecat.audio.krisp_instance`: PARTIAL | There is no JS binding for the Krisp SDK. The logic is ported against a KrispAudioSdk interface, and the default SDK is a stub whose getVersion/globalInit throw 'krisp_audio is not ported yet'. A binding can be installed with setKrispAudioSdk() (new API). KRISP_SAMPLE_RATES and KRISP_FRAME_DURATIONS map to enum member names, which are resolved against the installed SDK. The threading Lock was dropped because the JS methods are synchronous. The old-signature fallback catches TypeError only. | UNRESOLVED: audio/resamplers/soxr_resampler.ts and soxr_stream_resampler.ts: soxrResample and SoxrResampleStream are stubs that throw. - `pipecat.audio.turn.smart_turn._whisper_features`: No numpy: a direct 400-point real DFT with precomputed twiddle tables, reflect padding, a Slaney mel filterbank, and float32 normalization emulated with Math.fround. - `pipecat.audio.utils`: numpy was replaced with typed arrays. These were reimplemented in TS: audioop G.711 ulaw/alaw (CPython's algorithms; the smoke test output looks like standard G.711 values, but it was not compared against Python's audioop because audioop is missing from the local Python), the wave writer (pcmToWav), and BS.1770 integrated loudness (pyloudnorm-style K-weighting and gating, replacing the `loudness` package, so small numeric differences are possible). - `pipecat.bus.serializers.json`: PARTIAL | The wire format is upstream's. DIVERGENCES: (1) Python resolves the qualified class names in __type__ with importlib. TS uses a module-level registry instead, with two exported functions I added: registerSerializableType(qualifiedName, type) and registerEnumField(cls, field, enumObj). Registered by default: every class in frames/frames, bus/messages, bus/ui/messages, metrics/metrics and registry/types, plus LLMContext, LLMSpecificMessage, ToolsSchema, FunctionSchema, FrameDirection and ErrorCategory. An instance of an unregistered class is skipped with upstream's 'unserializable type' warning. (2) Dataclass field names are converted camelCase -> snake_case on the wire and back; plain dict keys are not changed. (3) An `as const` enum value cannot say which enum it belongs to, so plain-Enum fields have to be declared. BusFrameMessage.direction is declared by default. Class enums such as ErrorCategory are detected automatically. (4) Pydantic models and dataclasses are serialized the same way. (5) Deserialization passes all decoded fields to the options constructor, then assigns them to the instance (this covers init=False fields). (6) JSON.stringify does not escape non-ASCII the way Python's ensure_ascii does; the payloads parse to the same data. DIVERGENCE: ClassifierImage reads the MIME type from the file's signature bytes (PNG/JPEG/WebP accepted; GIF/BMP/TIFF/ICO recognized and rejected) instead of opening it with PIL. Errors are plain Error where upstream raises ValueError, KeyError or ValidationError. ScoreResult.probability uses a deep equality that ignores dict key order, like Python ==. yes_no/choice/score -> yesNo/choice/score with a trailing { images } options object. perf_counter -> monotonic(). ClassifierImage.fromFrame uses utils/image encodeImage, whose JPEG encoding of raw pixels is a stub upstream of this module. - `pipecat.frames.frames`: Continued below the PORTING marker in packages/core/src/frames/frames.ts, marker removed. Every non-deprecated frame and helper class is ported. Added mixins: FileRawFrame and a marker DTMFFrame. InputTextRawFrame extends SystemFrame, copies TextFrame's fields and uses addVirtualBase(TextFrame). Re-exports TextType because upstream rtvi/observer imports it through frames. Deprecated items dropped: UninterruptibleFrame; FatalErrorFrame; ErrorFrame.fatal (toString leaves out 'fatal:', and the RTVI port must send fatal=false); all StartFrame fields (audio_in/out_sample_rate, enable_metrics/tracing/usage_metrics, report_only_initial_ttfb, tracing_context), since processors read them from FrameProcessorSetup and StartFrame now has no fields; AggregatedTextFrame/AggregatedTextProgressFrame.aggregated_by; the TTSSpeakFrame(append_to_context=None) coercion; ServiceUpdateSettingsFrame.settings (the deprecated dict; only delta/service/reachInactiveServices kept); TaskFrame, TaskSystemFrame, EndTaskFrame, StopTaskFrame, CancelTaskFrame and InterruptionTaskFrame aliases; the AggregationType alias. Divergences: OutputDTMFFrame/OutputDTMFUrgentFrame.to_string() is renamed toDialString(), because toString() is __str__. fromString() rejects invalid characters with a plain Error (upstream raises ValueError from KeypadEntry). The LLMThoughtStartFrame and DTMF validations throw Error. toString prints JS null/true instead of None/True, and image sizes print as '(w, h)'. Still failing the typecheck: missing modules adapters/schemas/direct_function.ts and transcriptions/language.ts, which other agents have not written yet, plus TS6305 from the @pipedog/asyncio project reference (packages/asyncio/dist not built), which every importer of that package gets.
- `pipecat.frames.protobufs.frames_pb2`: DIVERGENCE: upstream is protoc-generated code that needs the protobuf runtime. This is a hand-written proto3 codec for the same frames.proto schema, with the same wire format. Messages: TextFrame, AudioRawFrame, TranscriptionFrame, MessageFrame, InterruptionFrame, Frame. Each has camelCase fields, serializeToString(), static fromString() and parseFromString(); Frame has whichOneof('frame'), and setting one oneof member clears the others. DESCRIPTOR.fieldsByName[...].messageType.fields lists each field's proto name and its TS property (attr). uint64 values are JS numbers, exact up to 2^53. Those packages also depend on @pipedog/core, so declaring them would create a dependency cycle and break the core-takes-no-deps rule. - `pipecat.observers.startup_timing_observer`: Dropped deprecated StartupWarmupTiming, StartupTimingObserver.on_startup_warmup and StartupTimingReport.warmup (with them the internal _warmup/_warmup_finished_ns; setup phase now ends at _setupFinishedNs, which is what upstream computes when warmup never fires). Pydantic models -> classes with options constructors.
- `pipecat.observers.turn_tracking_observer`: The deprecated max_frames parameter is dropped; the options are { turnEndTimeoutSecs }, and observeEveryPush is always false as upstream. The loop.call_later timer becomes a createTask (@pipedog/asyncio) that sleeps and then ends the turn. Once the sleep finishes it clears its own handle, so a later cancel no longer stops the callback, like a call_later handle that has fired. cleanup() cancels it. The only typecheck error is the shared TS6305 @pipedog/asyncio project-reference build issue.
- `pipecat.observers.user_bot_latency_observer`: Dropped the deprecated `maxFrames` option and `LatencyBreakdown.chronological_events()`. Pydantic models became classes with options constructors and camelCase fields. Private enums: _MomentKind is an as-const object; _Owner uses unique symbols so it can be told apart from literal string owners. The default timeSource is `time()` from @pipedog/asyncio. Typechecks clean.

- `pipecat.pipeline.base_pipeline`: Broad catches call rethrowIfCancelled. Uses gather() and monotonicNs(). Typechecks clean.
- `pipecat.pipeline.capabilities`: BotCapabilities is a class with camelCase fields. Added modelDump({excludeNone}) and toJSON() that output upstream's snake_case keys, because RTVI sends this object to the client in bot-ready.
- `pipecat.pipeline.job_context`: Dropped the deprecated resolve_job_params(). `async with` became enter()/exit(error) plus a run(fn) helper; `async for` became Symbol.asyncIterator. The asyncio.shield around cancelJobGroup is kept. Assumes BaseWorker (workers/base_worker.ts, not written yet) exposes `jobGroups: Map<string, JobGroup>`, `createJobGroupAndRequestJob(names, {params})` and `cancelJobGroup(id, {reason})`. JobGroup.responses is a Record keyed by worker name. Typechecks clean except for the missing base_worker import.
- `pipecat.pipeline.job_decorator`: Follows the style of the sibling worker_ready_decorator.ts: job({name, sequential}) works as a TC39 decorator or a plain function, and _collectJobHandlers returns a Map of bound methods. The bound methods keep the isJobHandler/jobName/jobSequential flags.
- `pipecat.pipeline.llm_switcher`: Dropped deprecated register_direct_function. strategy_type is passed as options.strategyType. It calls each member's protected _syncRegisteredToolHandlers through a cast. runInference forwards the LLMService.runInference options object.
- `pipecat.pipeline.llm_with_backend`: Uses the TS _BackendSession API from workers/llm/backend_llm_worker.ts (run()/async iterator/send/detached) in place of async with. BackendRequestStrategy class attributes are instance fields. RuntimeError/ValueError/assert become Error.
- `pipecat.pipeline.parallel_pipeline`: Variadic constructor(...args: FrameProcessor[][]).
- `pipecat.pipeline.pipeline`: Constructor signatures: `new Pipeline(processors, {source, sink})`, `new PipelineSource(fn, options)`, `new PipelineSink(fn, options)`. The required positional argument stays positional. Typechecks clean.
- `pipecat.pipeline.runner`: Whole module is deprecated: it only re-exports WorkerRunner (import it from workers/runner.ts) and holds the deprecated PipelineRunner alias. No file written.
- `pipecat.pipeline.service_switcher`: Dropped the deprecated ErrorFrame.fatal check in push_frame (the field isn't ported). strategy_type -> options.strategyType. dataclasses.replace(frame) -> shallow copy + renewFrameIdentity (metadata/pts are carried over rather than reset). The deque(maxlen=64) is an array capped at 64. The isUsable getter is overridden.
- `pipecat.pipeline.sync_parallel_pipeline`: frame_order keyword -> an optional trailing options object after the processor lists. FrameOrder is an as-const enum.
- `pipecat.pipeline.task`: Whole module is deprecated: it re-exports from pipeline/worker.ts and includes the deprecated PipelineTask alias. Import from pipeline/worker.ts instead. No file written.
- `pipecat.pipeline.worker`: Dropped (deprecated): PipelineTask, PipelineTaskParams, the toolResources option, the FrameProcessorSetup tool_resources field, the ErrorFrame.fatal branch, the StartFrame config fields (now `new StartFrame()`), and the legacy setup_pipeline_task hook name. Signature is `new PipelineWorker(pipeline, options)`. ProcessorUnusablePolicy is an as-const object; PipelineParams is a class. Calling `BaseWorker.cancel(self)` directly became `BaseWorker.prototype.cancel.call(this, ...)`. Assumed API of the unwritten workers/base_worker.ts: _finishedEvent, _children, bus, sendBusMessage, start/stop/end/cancel/activateWorker/onBusMessage, _handleWorkerEnd/_handleWorkerCancel, _propagate*ToChildren, _printDanglingTasks. rtvi/index.ts and rtvi/frames.ts are also not written yet. Against temporary stubs of those three modules (in a scratch copy, since deleted), the file typechecks with zero errors.
- `pipecat.pipeline.worker_observer`: Dropped deprecated on_startup_warmup / StartupWarmup handling. weakref.WeakValueDictionary of pushed frames is emulated with Map<id, WeakRef<Frame>> plus FinalizationRegistry (internal WeakFrameMap). The `Proxy` class keeps its upstream name and carries a biome-ignore because it shadows the global Proxy. The proxy tasks wait on asyncio Queue.get(), which is a cancellation point, so cancelTask() stops them cleanly. File: packages/core/src/pipeline/worker_observer.ts
- `pipecat.pipeline.worker_ready_decorator`: workerReady({ name }) returns a decorator that works both as a TC39 method decorator and as a plain call, e.g. `static { workerReady({name})(this.prototype.m) }`, because Node type-stripping does not run decorators. It follows the same pattern as the concurrently ported job_decorator.ts. _collectWorkerReadyHandlers walks the prototype chain and returns Map<string, boundFn> instead of a dict, and throws Error instead of ValueError.

- `pipecat.processors.aggregators`: Empty __init__.py -> index.ts with header and export {}.
- `pipecat.processors.aggregators.async_tool_messages`: The payload JSON is built to match Python json.dumps(ensure_ascii=False) byte for byte (', ' and ': ' separators), so contexts are identical across ports. AsyncToolMessagePayload is a frozen class (Object.freeze) with camelCase fields; wire keys are unchanged. Use it as a module namespace: import * as asyncToolMessages.
- `pipecat.processors.aggregators.dtmf_aggregator`: Positional timeout/termination_digit/prefix -> options object (DTMFAggregatorOptions).
- `pipecat.processors.aggregators.gated`: Constructor keeps upstream's positional params (gateOpenFn, gateCloseFn, startOpen, direction) because upstream has no keyword-only args. Added a GateFn type alias.
- `pipecat.processors.aggregators.gated_llm_context`: Kept upstream's `if` (not `elif`) after the StartFrame branch, so a StartFrame is pushed twice exactly as upstream does. A comment marks it. Imports BaseNotifier from utils/sync/base_notifier.ts, which another agent is porting.
- `pipecat.processors.aggregators.llm_context`: LLMStandardMessage and LLMContextToolChoice are structural TS types; upstream aliases them to OpenAI types for type-checking only. LLMContext takes an options object ({messages, tools, toolChoice}). Static create*Message helpers take options objects. getMessages(filter, {truncateLargeValues}). The asyncio.to_thread audio encoding runs inline. Base64 uses a local btoa helper. Deep copies use structuredClone. setMessages replaces the array in place, like Python's slice assignment. customTools may be a Map or a record. Deps: utils/image.encodeImage is a stub upstream of this module and cannot JPEG-encode raw pixels yet, so createImageMessage only works with images that are already encoded.
- `pipecat.processors.aggregators.llm_context_summarizer`: uuid4 becomes crypto.randomUUID(). The dedicated LLM's protected `_generateSummary` is called through a cast, as upstream calls it directly. `template.format(summary=...)` becomes a small helper that replaces {summary} and un-doubles {{ and }}. setup() takes FrameProcessorSetup, typed `| unknown` to match the BaseObject.setup signature.
- `pipecat.processors.aggregators.llm_response_universal`: Dropped deprecated params fields: LLMUserAggregatorParams.filter_incomplete_user_turns and user_turn_completion_config (and the FilterIncompleteUserTurnStrategies translation that depended on them), and LLMAssistantAggregatorParams.enable_context_summarization and context_summarization_config (with the LLMContextSummarizationConfig.to_auto_config path). Dataclasses become classes with options constructors. Python's `__iter__` on the pair becomes [Symbol.iterator], so destructured values are typed as a union of the two aggregators. `_userTurnController`, `_realtimeHandoffFlush` and `_realtimeHandoffFlushImmediate` are public (underscore names kept) because the paired assistant aggregator uses them, and TS protected would block that. VADController._vadAnalyzer is read through a cast. json.dumps(ensure_ascii=False) becomes a local helper with Python's ', ' and ': ' separators; floats like 1.0 still print as 1. Python truthiness of frame.result is copied by an isTruthy helper (empty dicts and lists are falsy). RuntimeError becomes Error. Marker constants are imported statically from turns/user_turn_completion_mixin.ts instead of upstream's lazy import.
- `pipecat.processors.aggregators.llm_text_processor`: PatternMatch is imported from utils/text/pattern_pair_aggregator.ts and SimpleTextAggregator from utils/text/simple_text_aggregator.ts; other agents own both modules.
- `pipecat.processors.async_generator`: Python truthiness check on serialized data (empty string/bytes are skipped). Imports FrameSerializer from serializers/base_serializer.ts.
- `pipecat.processors.audio`: Empty __init__.py -> index.ts with header and export {}.
- `pipecat.processors.audio.audio_buffer_processor`: Dropped deprecated on_user_turn_audio_data / on_bot_turn_audio_data events (1.8.0) and the addEventHandler override that warned about them; their internal run-folding (_endUserRun/_endBotRun) is kept. bytearray buffers are an exported growable ByteBuffer helper.
- `pipecat.processors.audio.vad_processor`: Reads startSecs/stopSecs from the vadAnalyzer passed in (the same object as upstream's controller._vad_analyzer), so it does not depend on VADController internals. on_broadcast_frame kwargs arrive as one options object.
- `pipecat.processors.filters`: Empty __init__.py -> index.ts with header and export {}.
- `pipecat.processors.filters.frame_filter`: The positional `types` tuple is now the options object { types: FrameClass[] }.
- `pipecat.processors.filters.function_filter`: Positional filter/direction/filter_system_frames become the options object { filter, direction, filterSystemFrames }. direction is DOWNSTREAM when undefined, and an explicit null filters both directions.
- `pipecat.processors.filters.wake_check_filter`: The whole module is deprecated (WakeCheckFilter, since 0.0.106; replaced by WakePhraseUserTurnStartStrategy), so no TS file was written.
- `pipecat.processors.filters.wake_notifier_filter`: The positional notifier is now the options object { notifier, types, filter }.
- `pipecat.processors.frame_processor`: hand-ported reference
- `pipecat.processors.frameworks`: Empty __init__.py -> index.ts with header and export {}.
- `pipecat.processors.frameworks.rtvi`: index.ts re-exports frames, models (BotOutputTransformResult, SpokenProgressData), observer and processor, as upstream __all__ does.
- `pipecat.processors.frameworks.rtvi.frames`: All frames, including RTVIConfigureObserverFrame. RTVIFunctionCallReportLevel is imported type-only from observer.ts to avoid a circular import. In toString(), object values are JSON.stringify'd where Python prints a dict repr.
- `pipecat.processors.frameworks.rtvi.models`: Pydantic models become classes on a small internal BaseModel (no zod dependency). Fields are camelCase via `declare` plus a static field table. Constructors validate camelCase options; static modelValidate() validates wire snake_case data, recursively. modelDump({excludeNone}) and toJSON() emit the upstream snake_case keys. extra='allow' models (A11yNode, A11ySelection, A11ySnapshot) keep unknown keys in modelExtra. Exports a ValidationError (name 'ValidationError'), which processor.ts's isValidationError already recognizes. Additions with no upstream counterpart in this module: ValidationError, BaseModel, ModelDumpOptions, SignalMessageOptions / TextMessageOptions / AudioLevelMessageOptions helper types. Dropped (deprecated): LLMFunctionCallMessageData and LLMFunctionCallMessage, which only the deprecated RTVIProcessor.handle_function_call uses. Kept although deprecated, because the observer still sends them by default and clients read them: BotLLMTextMessage, BotTTSTextMessage, UserTranscriptionMessage/Data, and BotOutputMessageData.aggregatedBy together with its text_type sync validator. Divergence: validation is strict and does not do pydantic's lax coercions (for example the string '5' is not accepted as an int). Remaining tsc error at observer.ts:651 belongs to the observer port: it passes a `{}`-typed args value to LLMFunctionCallInProgressMessageData.arguments, which expects Record<string, unknown>.
- `pipecat.processors.frameworks.rtvi.observer`: Not ported because upstream deprecates them: RTVIObserverParams.userTranscriptionEnabled and skipAggregatorTypes, the aggregationType argument of add/removeBotOutputTransformer, and the 2-parameter (text, textType) transform signature. Every transform is now called with 4 arguments. With userTranscriptionEnabled gone, user-transcription is always sent, which is the upstream default. The deprecated wire messages bot-llm-text, bot-tts-text, bot-transcription and user-transcription are still sent because current clients consume them. models.ts is not written yet, so this assumes RTVI models are classes with camelCase constructor options and a modelDump({ excludeNone }) method that returns snake_case wire keys. metrics.ts has no modelDump, so a local helper dumps metrics to snake_case and drops nulls. Loguru's async sink becomes logger.addSink, which runs each send as a tracked createTask. Every broad catch calls rethrowIfCancelled. tsc: the only errors left come from missing rtvi/frames.ts and rtvi/models.ts (unresolved imports and the instanceof narrowing that depends on them). | UNRESOLVED: processor.ts base64ToBytes uses atob, which differs from Python's base64.b64decode at the edges: Python rejects missing padding and silently drops non-alphabet characters, while atob accepts missing padding and throws on non-alphabet characters. Exact parity would need a hand-written decoder. I left it as is. | UNRESOLVED: observer.ts _handleContext: upstream fails on a content-part list that holds a string item or a non-string text (TypeError, caught and logged), while the TS skips such items or converts them to strings. This only happens with malformed context, so I did not mirror it. | UNRESOLVED: There are no TS tests yet for rtvi/observer, rtvi/processor, frame_processor_metrics, registry or ai_service under packages/core/test, so the fixes were checked only with typecheck and biome, which pass for the reviewed files. | UNRESOLVED: I found no divergences in registry/registry.ts, registry/types.ts, services/ai_service.ts or processors/metrics/frame_processor_metrics.ts. The `||` uses in metrics (start_time or time.time()) copy Python's falsy `or` on purpose. ai_service.ts rightly leaves out the deprecated _warn_init_param_moved_to_settings, and every broad catch calls rethrowIfCancelled.
- `pipecat.processors.frameworks.rtvi.processor`: Not ported because upstream deprecates them: the transport constructor option and handleFunctionCall(). ErrorFrame.fatal is deprecated and not ported, so RTVI error messages always send fatal: false. This file adds an RTVIModelLike interface ({ modelDump(options) }) and isValidationError() (matches error.name ValidationError or ZodError) to stand in for pydantic BaseModel and ValidationError. models.ts does not exist yet, so the assumed API is Model.modelValidate(obj), camelCase fields such as msgId, runImmediately and jobId, and instanceof checks against FileBytes and FileUrl. Base64 is decoded inline with atob (asyncio.to_thread is not needed). Client version parsing follows Python int() rules. tsc: the only errors left are imports of rtvi/frames.ts, rtvi/models.ts and index.ts (for version()), none of which exist yet. | UNRESOLVED: processor.ts base64ToBytes uses atob, which differs from Python's base64.b64decode at the edges: Python rejects missing padding and silently drops non-alphabet characters, while atob accepts missing padding and throws on non-alphabet characters. - `pipecat.registry`: registry/index.ts re-exports WorkerRegistry from ./registry.ts. | UNRESOLVED: audio/resamplers/soxr_resampler.ts and soxr_stream_resampler.ts: soxrResample and SoxrResampleStream are stubs that throw. So createFileResampler/createStreamResampler, ulawToPcm/pcmToUlaw/alawToPcm/pcmToAlaw at different rates, loadDtmfAudio at any rate other than 8000, and LocalSmartTurnAnalyzerV3 at any rate other than 16 kHz all throw at runtime. This needs an injected libsoxr WASM build or a pure-TS resampler fallback in core, which is a design decision beyond a parity fix. | UNRESOLVED: audio/krisp_instance.ts: the krisp_audio SDK is a stub until setKrispAudioSdk() is called. LocalSmartTurnAnalyzerV3 needs an injected createSession (an ONNX runtime), and the PIPECAT_SMART_TURN_LOG_DATA WAV dump is a stub that only logs a warning because core has no filesystem. | UNRESOLVED: adapters/schemas/direct_function.ts: upstream sets each parameter's 'description' from the docstring. The TS metadata (DirectFunctionMetadata) has no per-parameter description field, so descriptions only appear if they are put inside the JSON-schema hint. Also, DirectFunctionWrapper.invoke silently drops extra argument names, where Python's function(params=..., **args) raises TypeError. Fixing either would mean inventing API, so I left both. | UNRESOLVED: audio/turn/smart_turn/base_smart_turn.ts: an edge case remains. If sampleRate is 0, maxSamples is 0; Python's segment_audio[-0:] keeps the whole array, while the TS slice gives an empty one. - `pipecat.services.llm_service`: packages/core/src/services/llm_service.ts. LLMService<TAdapter> extends UserTurnCompletionLLMServiceMixin(AIService); WebsocketLLMService<TAdapter> extends WebsocketService(LLMService)<TAdapter>. Deprecated APIs left out: FunctionCallParams.tool_resources, register_direct_function, unregister_direct_function (and its internal _unregister_direct_function, which nothing else used), the enable_async_tool_cancellation option (so _isCancellable checks only cancellableByLlm), _warn_turn_completion_settings_are_strategy_owned, and the legacy LLMUpdateSettingsFrame(settings={...}) dict path. The internal _registerDirectFunction is kept because auto-registration uses it. New in TS: an exported NotImplementedError class (Python's builtin), since runInference throws it and _generateSummary catches it. Python bound methods compare equal; JS closures don't, so the done callback, the missing-function handler and the cancel-tool handler are stored once as # fields so identity checks still match. On cancellation: when a call is cancelled (interruption, timeout or the LLM's cancel tool), its handler's task is cancelled through @pipedog/asyncio. The JSDoc tells handlers to pass currentSignal() to fetch and other foreign awaits, or wrap them in abortable(), since those awaits cannot be stopped from outside otherwise. The toolOptions attributes on handlers are read in camelCase (_pipecatCancelOnInterruption, _pipecatTimeoutSecs, _pipecatCancellableByLlm, _pipecatCleanup); log lines show the snake_case option names. Small differences: log lines use JSON.stringify where Python uses repr or json.dumps (different spacing), and a ConnectionError is a plain Error. Python 'or' semantics kept where it matters (a 0 timeout falls back to the default). Typecheck is clean and a smoke test (construct, registerFunction, system-instruction composition) passed. | UNRESOLVED: packages/core/src/services/llm_service.ts: `bunx biome check` reports formatting differences (for example the `_cancelFunctionCall` call layout around line 2485). They are layout only, not behavior, and the task said not to restyle correct code, so I left them. Running `bunx biome format --write` on the file would fix them. | UNRESOLVED: Not runtime-checked: `bun run typecheck` passes, but packages/core has no test directory, so none of these four modules were exercised by tests. | UNRESOLVED: Reviewed and left as-is (correct ports or allowed omissions): settings.ts and tests/utils.ts match upstream with no real divergences. websocket_service.ts matches too: every broad catch calls rethrowIfCancelled, CancelledError is rethrown first in `_receiveTaskHandler`, `??` defaults and durations in seconds are kept, and the backoff argument order is right. In llm_service.ts, `||` is used on purpose in two places to copy Python `or`: the summarization timeout and the per-tool vs global function-call timeout. The deprecated APIs were correctly left out and are listed in the file header: register/unregister_direct_function, tool_resources, enable_async_tool_cancellation, the turn-completion warnings and the legacy settings dict path. The filterIncompleteUserTurns and userTurnCompletionConfig settings fields are kept because the user turn strategy still uses them. | UNRESOLVED: On the side agent's note about stopping in-progress work on interruption: this port stops an interrupted function call by cancelling the task running it, using @pipedog/asyncio. That only takes effect when the handler reaches an await on a cancellable primitive. A handler stuck on an outside await (fetch, an SDK, a WebSocket) keeps running unless it is passed `currentSignal()` or wrapped in `abortable()`. The header of llm_service.ts says this, but the rule is not enforced in code. This review found no step that misses the stop signal; whether to use Effect for the core is a separate design decision.

- `pipecat.services.settings`: packages/core/src/services/settings.ts. dataclasses.fields() is the instance's own keys minus 'extra'. Because of useDefineForClassFields, every settings subclass that declares fields must end its constructor with this._assignOptions(options); this is documented in the module docs. An option left undefined stays NOT_GIVEN, while null is a real value. Python == becomes structural equality (exported as settingsValuesEqual), and copy.deepcopy becomes a deep copy that keeps prototypes and the NOT_GIVEN singleton. fromMapping also matches snake_case keys to camelCase fields, and TTSSettings._aliases accepts both voice_id and voiceId. fromMapping itself is not deprecated, so it is kept. LLMSettings keeps filterIncompleteUserTurns and userTurnCompletionConfig: setting them from application code is deprecated upstream, but the user turn strategy still drives turn completion through them. Smoke-tested applyUpdate, fromMapping, copy and givenFields. | UNRESOLVED: packages/core/src/services/llm_service.ts: `bunx biome check` reports formatting differences (for example the `_cancelFunctionCall` call layout around line 2485). WebsocketTTSService = WebsocketService(TTSService) mixin. Only remaining tsc errors come from modules that aren't ported yet: utils/context/aggregated_frame_sequencer.ts and utils/text/transforms/pronunciations.ts. Assumed APIs for them: new AggregatedFrameSequencer({name, streaming}); registerSpoken(frame, contextId, ttsText, appendToContext, {buildTracker, includesInterFrameSpaces, language}); processWord(word, pts, contextId, includesInterFrameSpaces); forceComplete(contextId, lastPts); clear({keepContexts}); pronunciationTransform(pronunciations, formatter, {serviceName}). Text transforms are typed as functions and pronunciation transforms are detected with instanceof PronunciationTransform, which assumes that port makes its instances callable. - `pipecat.services.websocket_service`: packages/core/src/services/websocket_service.ts. WebsocketService is a defineMixin mixin, and its members are public because declaration emit rejects private or protected members in class expressions. Upstream's websockets library has no JS equivalent, so this file adds WebsocketClientConnection over the global WebSocket (send, recv, async iteration, ping, close with closeTimeout, state), plus websocketConnect(uri, {closeTimeout, openTimeout, additionalHeaders, subprotocols, webSocketFactory}), the State constants, and the ConnectionClosed, ConnectionClosedOK and ConnectionClosedError errors. recv() and the opening handshake are cancellation points. _BoundedCloseConnection's log for an unacknowledged close is built into close(). Divergences: ping() only checks the socket is open, because the WebSocket API cannot send ping frames. Headers work with the Bun, Node (undici) and Deno WebSocket; Cloudflare Workers needs an injected webSocketFactory (fetch with an Upgrade header). ReportErrorCallback takes { forceTreatAsPermanent } as an options object, matching pushErrorFrame. exponentialBackoffTime is called with positional arguments, which is the signature the ported utils/network.ts has. | UNRESOLVED: packages/core/src/services/llm_service.ts: `bunx biome check` reports formatting differences (for example the `_cancelFunctionCall` call layout around line 2485). They are layout only, not behavior, and the task said not to restyle correct code, so I left them. Running `bunx biome format --write` on the file would fix them. | UNRESOLVED: Not runtime-checked: `bun run typecheck` passes, but packages/core has no test directory, so none of these four modules were exercised by tests. | UNRESOLVED: Reviewed and left as-is (correct ports or allowed omissions): settings.ts and tests/utils.ts match upstream with no real divergences. websocket_service.ts matches too: every broad catch calls rethrowIfCancelled, CancelledError is rethrown first in `_receiveTaskHandler`, `??` defaults and durations in seconds are kept, and the backoff argument order is right. In llm_service.ts, `||` is used on purpose in two places to copy Python `or`: the summarization timeout and the per-tool vs global function-call timeout. The deprecated APIs were correctly left out and are listed in the file header: register/unregister_direct_function, tool_resources, enable_async_tool_cancellation, the turn-completion warnings and the legacy settings dict path. The filterIncompleteUserTurns and userTurnCompletionConfig settings fields are kept because the user turn strategy still uses them. | UNRESOLVED: On the side agent's note about stopping in-progress work on interruption: this port stops an interrupted function call by cancelling the task running it, using @pipedog/asyncio. That only takes effect when the handler reaches an await on a cancellable primitive. A handler stuck on an outside await (fetch, an SDK, a WebSocket) keeps running unless it is passed `currentSignal()` or wrapped in `abortable()`. The header of llm_service.ts says this, but the rule is not enforced in code. This review found no step that misses the stop signal; whether to use Effect for the core is a separate design decision.

Running the module fails with "Cannot find module '@pipedog/bus-adapters/index'". Those packages also depend on @pipedog/core, so declaring them would create a dependency cycle and break the core-takes-no-deps rule. The likely fix is to keep bus/adapters (base, llm_context_adapter, tools_schema_adapter) in core, but that is a manifest/packaging decision I was told not to make. I checked it with temporary symlinks, which I then removed: with the packages linked, serialize/deserialize round-trips and tsc reports no errors in the reviewed files. | UNRESOLVED: Minor, left as is: Python's json.dumps escapes non-ASCII and JSON.stringify does not, so the wire bytes differ but parse to the same values. - `pipecat.transcriptions.language`: Language StrEnum -> as-const object generated from upstream (327 codes, comments kept). Added isLanguage() helper to replace Python's Language(value) ValueError check in resolveLanguage. languageMap typed Partial<Record<Language,string>>. | UNRESOLVED: packages/core/src/transports/base_output.ts MediaSender._resizeImage (around line 1008) is a stub that throws "not ported yet: image resizing (PIL Image.resize)". Upstream's _draw_image resizes with PIL (Image.frombytes(...).resize(desired_size), which defaults to BICUBIC and premultiplies alpha for RGBA). So in TS, any OutputImageRawFrame with a format whose size differs from videoOutWidth x videoOutHeight makes the video task throw, where upstream resizes it. I did not write a substitute resampler: PIL is not installed here, so its output could not be checked pixel for pixel, and RGBA premultiply rounding depends on the Pillow version. This needs an owner decision: either a pure-JS bicubic resampler copied from Pillow's Resample.c (22-bit fixed-point coefficients), or a resizer the transport injects.

- `pipecat.transports.base_input`: Deprecated start_audio_in_streaming() dropped (subclasses override _startAudioInStreaming, triggered by InputTransportStartAudioStreamingFrame). Constructor is (params, options). _audioInQueue is initialized eagerly (upstream creates it only in _create_audio_task, so pushing audio earlier raised AttributeError) and is recreated with the task. resultCallback (typed unknown in frames.ts) is cast to a function before calling. | UNRESOLVED: packages/core/src/transports/base_output.ts MediaSender._resizeImage (around line 1008) is a stub that throws "not ported yet: image resizing (PIL Image.resize)". Upstream's _draw_image resizes with PIL (Image.frombytes(...).resize(desired_size), which defaults to BICUBIC and premultiplies alpha for RGBA). So in TS, any OutputImageRawFrame with a format whose size differs from videoOutWidth x videoOutHeight makes the video task throw, where upstream resizes it. I did not write a substitute resampler: PIL is not installed here, so its output could not be checked pixel for pixel, and RGBA premultiply rounding depends on the Pillow version. This needs an owner decision: either a pure-JS bicubic resampler copied from Pillow's Resample.c (22-bit fixed-point coefficients), or a resizer the transport injects.
- `pipecat.transports.base_output`: PARTIAL | PIL image resizing in MediaSender._drawImage is a stub: MediaSender._resizeImage throws 'not ported yet' when an image with a format has a size other than videoOutWidth x videoOutHeight. Same-size or format-less images pass through unchanged. The ThreadPoolExecutor is dropped (no threads). The deprecated video_out_bitrate warning is not ported. The nested class is exported as MediaSender and is also available as BaseOutputTransport.MediaSender. _mediaSenders is a Map keyed by string|null. itertools.cycle is a local generator. The write in _internalWriteAudioFrame is wrapped in abortable() inside waitFor, so the audioOutWriteTimeoutSecs timeout can actually interrupt a foreign await that never returns. Broad catch in _audioTaskHandler calls rethrowIfCancelled. Running the module fails with "Cannot find module '@pipedog/bus-adapters/index'". Those packages also depend on @pipedog/core, so declaring them would create a dependency cycle and break the core-takes-no-deps rule. The likely fix is to keep bus/adapters (base, llm_context_adapter, tools_schema_adapter) in core, but that is a manifest/packaging decision I was told not to make. I checked it with temporary symlinks, which I then removed: with the packages linked, serialize/deserialize round-trips and tsc reports no errors in the reviewed files. | UNRESOLVED: Minor, left as is: Python's json.dumps escapes non-ASCII and JSON.stringify does not, so the wire bytes differ but parse to the same values. JSONMessageSerializer serializes a dataclass from Object.entries, not dataclasses.fields, and passes every decoded field to the constructor instead of splitting init and non-init fields. str()/repr() of None, floats and bools in classifier error messages and in OpenAI Decisions probability keys does not match Python exactly (for example 'None' vs '', 1.0 vs 1).

- `pipecat.turns.speculation_gate`: SpeculationState is an as-const enum. GatedFrame is the tuple [Frame, FrameDirection]. Constructor takes {name}. Members are protected _camelCase. | UNRESOLVED: packages/core/src/transports/base_output.ts MediaSender._resizeImage (around line 1008) is a stub that throws "not ported yet: image resizing (PIL Image.resize)". Upstream's _draw_image resizes with PIL (Image.frombytes(...).resize(desired_size), which defaults to BICUBIC and premultiplies alpha for RGBA). So in TS, any OutputImageRawFrame with a format whose size differs from videoOutWidth x videoOutHeight makes the video task throw, where upstream resizes it. I did not write a substitute resampler: PIL is not installed here, so its output could not be checked pixel for pixel, and RGBA premultiply rounding depends on the Pillow version. This needs an owner decision: either a pure-JS bicubic resampler copied from Pillow's Resample.c (22-bit fixed-point coefficients), or a resizer the transport injects.
- `pipecat.turns.types`: ProcessFrameResult is an as-const enum. - `pipecat.turns.user_mute.mute_until_first_bot_complete_user_mute_strategy`: UNRESOLVED: packages/core/src/utils/yaml.ts: addIncludeConstructor/includeLoader are interface-only stubs that always throw. Upstream builds on PyYAML loader classes and reads included files from disk, but core takes no YAML dependency and has no filesystem access on Workers. The behavior needs an injected YAML parser and file reader, or a separate package; that is a design decision outside this review. | UNRESOLVED: packages/core/src/utils/http.ts: TIMEOUT_EXCEPTIONS is [TimeoutError] (from @pipedog/asyncio) and connectionLimits() returns a plain object instead of an httpx Limits. These are necessary divergences (JS has no httpx families) and are marked DIVERGENCE in the file. isTimeoutException() is a small extra helper with no upstream counterpart.
- `pipecat.turns.user_start`: Re-export index. Upstream sets KrispVivaIPUserTurnStartStrategy to None when the krisp import fails; here it is always re-exported from its core module. | UNRESOLVED: packages/core/src/transports/base_output.ts MediaSender._resizeImage (around line 1008) is a stub that throws "not ported yet: image resizing (PIL Image.resize)". Upstream's _draw_image resizes with PIL (Image.frombytes(...).resize(desired_size), which defaults to BICUBIC and premultiplies alpha for RGBA). So in TS, any OutputImageRawFrame with a format whose size differs from videoOutWidth x videoOutHeight makes the video task throw, where upstream resizes it. I did not write a substitute resampler: PIL is not installed here, so its output could not be checked pixel for pixel, and RGBA premultiply rounding depends on the Pillow version. This needs an owner decision: either a pure-JS bicubic resampler copied from Pillow's Resample.c (22-bit fixed-point coefficients), or a resizer the transport injects.
- `pipecat.turns.user_start.base_user_turn_start_strategy`: Dropped deprecated enable_user_speaking_frames ctor arg, deprecated reset() hook and __init_subclass__ reset warning; handleUserTurnStarted is now a plain no-op. broadcastFrame(FrameCls, options) mirrors FrameProcessor.broadcastFrame; processFrame returns null for upstream None. | UNRESOLVED: krisp_viva_ip_user_turn_start_strategy.ts: `_createIpSession` is still a stub that throws, because krisp_instance.ts's injectable KrispAudioSdk has no IpFloat or IpSessionConfig surface. The `os.path.isfile` model check is also missing, since core has no filesystem access. Both are documented in the file. Everything else matches upstream: buffer math, int16-to-float conversion, Math.trunc for int(), threshold logic and SDK acquire/release. | UNRESOLVED: file_storage.ts: LocalFileStorage save/load/delete/_trim disk I/O is a documented stub, because core cannot import node:fs. URL validation (_resolveSuffix) is ported faithfully. | UNRESOLVED: image.ts: JPEG encoding of raw pixels (Pillow) is a documented stub. Only already-encoded images ('image/...') pass through. | UNRESOLVED: wake_phrase_user_turn_start_strategy.ts: Python's `\b` is emulated with Unicode lookarounds `(?<![\p{L}\p{N}\p{M}_])`. This differs from `\b` only for phrases that begin or end with a non-word character, and such a phrase can never match stripped text either way. Left as is. | UNRESOLVED: Minor log-format difference, not changed: float durations print as JS numbers (e.g. `1s` instead of Python's `1.0s`) in the speech_timeout, turn_analyzer and eager strategies' warning and debug lines. | UNRESOLVED: Reviewed with no divergence found: all 7 user_start and 9 user_stop modules plus both index.ts files, async_tool_cancellation, deprecation (deprecated warn_deprecated_read, renamed_init_field and the deprecated re-export are correctly not ported), env, errors, file_resolver, log_config and network. Checks covered: event registrations (sync flags), `??` defaults, cancellation handling (CancelledError/TimeoutError-only catches rethrow everything else; fetch uses currentSignal plus abortable), integer division, seconds-based timers, Uint8Array math and license headers. `tsc -p packages/core` reports 0 errors. A scratch smoke test of the wake-phrase regex, NormalizedMatch casefolding and backoff overflow passed.

- `pipecat.turns.user_turn_completion_mixin`: UserTurnCompletionLLMServiceMixin is a defineMixin class factory: LLMService becomes `extends UserTurnCompletionLLMServiceMixin(AIService)`. The base is cast to Ctor<FrameProcessor> internally because defineMixin requires `<B extends Ctor>`. Its _-prefixed members are public on the mixin interface (they are protected upstream). TurnMarker and IncompleteType are as-const enums. UserTurnCompletionConfig is a class with getters (markers, completionInstructions, shortPrompt, longPrompt). The prompt render functions are module-private, as upstream. CancelledError is swallowed in _incompleteTimeoutHandler, as upstream. | UNRESOLVED: packages/core/src/transports/base_output.ts MediaSender._resizeImage (around line 1008) is a stub that throws "not ported yet: image resizing (PIL Image.resize)". Upstream's _draw_image resizes with PIL (Image.frombytes(...).resize(desired_size), which defaults to BICUBIC and premultiplies alpha for RGBA). So in TS, any OutputImageRawFrame with a format whose size differs from videoOutWidth x videoOutHeight makes the video task throw, where upstream resizes it. I did not write a substitute resampler: PIL is not installed here, so its output could not be checked pixel for pixel, and RGBA premultiply rounding depends on the Pillow version. This needs an owner decision: either a pure-JS bicubic resampler copied from Pillow's Resample.c (22-bit fixed-point coefficients), or a resizer the transport injects.
- `pipecat.turns.user_turn_controller`: Strategy event handlers are arrow-function fields, so the same reference is added in _setupStrategies and removed in _cleanupStrategies (Python bound methods compare equal). on_broadcast_frame forwards (frameCls, options). | UNRESOLVED: packages/core/src/services/tts_service.ts _transformText: upstream PronunciationTransform is a class with an async `__call__`, called directly as `await transform(text, text_type)`. The TS port calls each textTransforms entry as a function and checks `instanceof PronunciationTransform`, and `TextTransformFunction` does not include PronunciationTransform. utils/text/transforms/pronunciations.ts is not ported yet. If it is ported as a plain class with a method (not a callable function object), the call fails and the types reject passing `pronunciationTransformIpa()` output in textTransforms. Whoever ports pronunciations.ts needs to make PronunciationTransform callable, or tts_service.ts must be changed to match. | UNRESOLVED: Reviewed with no divergences found: the 15 empty-__init__ index.ts files (services ultravox/whisper/xai/xtts; transports daily/heygen/lemonslice/livekit/local/moq/smallwebrtc/tavus/vonage/websocket/whatsapp), turns/user_mute/index.ts, the base/always/first_speech user mute strategies, empty_user_turn.ts, eager_end_of_turn_mixin.ts, user_idle_controller.ts, user_turn_controller.ts, video_in_sampler.ts, and the rest of tts_service.ts, including WebsocketTTSService and InterruptibleTTSService. Deprecated APIs are correctly left out, broad catches call rethrowIfCancelled, and TimeoutError-only catches rethrow everything else.
- `pipecat.turns.user_turn_processor`: UNRESOLVED: packages/core/src/services/tts_service.ts _transformText: upstream PronunciationTransform is a class with an async `__call__`, called directly as `await transform(text, text_type)`. - `pipecat.turns.user_turn_strategies`: Dataclass __post_init__ chain is emulated with a protected _postInit(), which runs once from the most derived of the four strategy classes in the prototype chain (runsPostInit helper). This keeps upstream's override and super semantics: ExternalUserTurnStrategies does not apply the defaults. LocalSmartTurnAnalyzerV3 is imported statically, where upstream imports it lazily inside defaultUserTurnStopStrategies(). | UNRESOLVED: packages/core/src/transports/base_output.ts MediaSender._resizeImage (around line 1008) is a stub that throws "not ported yet: image resizing (PIL Image.resize)". Upstream's _draw_image resizes with PIL (Image.frombytes(...).resize(desired_size), which defaults to BICUBIC and premultiplies alpha for RGBA). So in TS, any OutputImageRawFrame with a format whose size differs from videoOutWidth x videoOutHeight makes the video task throw, where upstream resizes it. I did not write a substitute resampler: PIL is not installed here, so its output could not be checked pixel for pixel, and RGBA premultiply rounding depends on the Pillow version. This needs an owner decision: either a pure-JS bicubic resampler copied from Pillow's Resample.c (22-bit fixed-point coefficients), or a resizer the transport injects.
- `pipecat.utils`: Empty upstream __init__.py; index.ts is `export {}`. | UNRESOLVED: packages/core/src/bus/serializers/json.ts imports `@pipedog/bus-adapters/index` (LLMContextAdapter, ToolsSchemaAdapter), and that import does not resolve. @pipedog/core's package.json does not declare bus-adapters, bus-base, bus-llm-context-adapter or bus-tools-schema-adapter, and core's node_modules links only asyncio. Running the module fails with "Cannot find module '@pipedog/bus-adapters/index'". Those packages also depend on @pipedog/core, so declaring them would create a dependency cycle and break the core-takes-no-deps rule. The likely fix is to keep bus/adapters (base, llm_context_adapter, tools_schema_adapter) in core, but that is a manifest/packaging decision I was told not to make. I checked it with temporary symlinks, which I then removed: with the packages linked, serialize/deserialize round-trips and tsc reports no errors in the reviewed files. | UNRESOLVED: Minor, left as is: Python's json.dumps escapes non-ASCII and JSON.stringify does not, so the wire bytes differ but parse to the same values. JSONMessageSerializer serializes a dataclass from Object.entries, not dataclasses.fields, and passes every decoded field to the constructor instead of splitting init and non-init fields. str()/repr() of None, floats and bools in classifier error messages and in OpenAI Decisions probability keys does not match Python exactly (for example 'None' vs '', 1.0 vs 1).

A null content is formatted as 'None', matching upstream str(None). In the PAIR name lookbehind and in KNOWN_TOKENS (`\b`, `github_pat_\w{82,}`), a credential name or token right after a non-ASCII letter can be redacted in JS where Python would leave it. This only over-redacts and is harmless, so I left it unchanged. | UNRESOLVED: packages/core/src/workers/base_worker.ts checkWorkerNames: the TypeError message names JS types ("number") where Python names its own ("int"). It only affects the error text. | UNRESOLVED: packages/core/src/utils/string.ts: the default sentence splitter (Intl.Segmenter plus an abbreviation list) is a documented replacement for sentencex, so some sentence boundaries can differ from upstream. Install a sentencex-compatible splitter with setSentenceSegmenter() to get exact parity. This is a design gap, not a line-level porting bug.

- `pipecat.utils.security.redaction`: Regexes translated to JS syntax (named groups, \k<> backreferences, lookbehinds). Output is byte-identical to Python on a sample set covering pairs, URL passwords, known tokens and PEM keys. Minor difference: JS \w and \b are ASCII-only, while Python's are Unicode-aware. | UNRESOLVED: packages/core/src/utils/security/redaction.ts: JS `\w` and `\b` only match ASCII, while Python's match Unicode. In the PAIR name lookbehind and in KNOWN_TOKENS (`\b`, `github_pat_\w{82,}`), a credential name or token right after a non-ASCII letter can be redacted in JS where Python would leave it. This only over-redacts and is harmless, so I left it unchanged. | UNRESOLVED: packages/core/src/workers/base_worker.ts checkWorkerNames: the TypeError message names JS types ("number") where Python names its own ("int"). It only affects the error text. | UNRESOLVED: packages/core/src/utils/string.ts: the default sentence splitter (Intl.Segmenter plus an abbreviation list) is a documented replacement for sentencex, so some sentence boundaries can differ from upstream. Install a sentencex-compatible splitter with setSentenceSegmenter() to get exact parity. This is a design gap, not a line-level porting bug.
- `pipecat.utils.security.ssrf`: Includes a small port of the Python ipaddress features this module needs: IPNetwork (CIDR with strict host bits, contains()), ipAddress() and ipAddressIsGlobal(), using the IANA private and shared registries and judging IPv4-mapped IPv6 by its IPv4 address, as Python 3.13 does. Host resolution is pluggable (setHostResolver). The default resolves IP literals directly and uses node:dns through process.getBuiltinModule when the runtime has it, with no static node: import. A host that cannot be resolved is BLOCKED, as with upstream's OSError. allowedNetworks takes IPNetwork[]. | UNRESOLVED: packages/core/src/utils/security/redaction.ts: JS `\w` and `\b` only match ASCII, while Python's match Unicode. In the PAIR name lookbehind and in KNOWN_TOKENS (`\b`, `github_pat_\w{82,}`), a credential name or token right after a non-ASCII letter can be redacted in JS where Python would leave it. This only over-redacts and is harmless, so I left it unchanged. | UNRESOLVED: packages/core/src/workers/base_worker.ts checkWorkerNames: the TypeError message names JS types ("number") where Python names its own ("int"). - `pipecat.utils.shared`: acquires(name) and releases(name) work as TC39 decorators or plain wrappers (e.g. this.prototype.setup = acquires('client')(this.prototype.setup)), because Node type-stripping doesn't run decorators. Per-instance state is kept in a WeakMap instead of the instance's __dict__. Returns null where upstream returns None. | UNRESOLVED: packages/core/src/utils/yaml.ts: addIncludeConstructor/includeLoader are interface-only stubs that always throw. - `pipecat.utils.startup`: Setup files are ES modules loaded with dynamic import() (file: URLs resolved against process.cwd(); the path separator is ';' on win32, ':' elsewhere). runSetupHook({ target, functionName }) also accepts the camelCase export name (setupWorkerRunner). Dropped the deprecated deprecated_function_name fallback (setup_pipeline_task). Does nothing on runtimes without env vars or a file system (Workers). | UNRESOLVED: packages/core/src/utils/security/redaction.ts: JS `\w` and `\b` only match ASCII, while Python's match Unicode. Install a sentencex-compatible splitter with setSentenceSegmenter() to get exact parity. This is a design gap, not a line-level porting bug.
- `pipecat.utils.string`: PARTIAL | sentencex is a native extension with no edge-compatible JS build. Segmentation goes through a pluggable SentenceSegmenter (setSentenceSegmenter). The default uses Intl.Segmenter, merges splits after a list of common abbreviations and dotted acronyms, and passed every upstream test case tried; it is not guaranteed to match sentencex on every input. Install a sentencex-compatible segmenter for exact parity. Offsets are JS string indices (UTF-16), not Python code points. matchEndofsentence(text, { language }). Python frozensets are ReadonlySet; StartEndTags is a readonly tuple. | UNRESOLVED: packages/core/src/utils/security/redaction.ts: JS `\w` and `\b` only match ASCII, while Python's match Unicode. All upstream test_markdown_text_filter cases pass. A 34-case differential run against Python-Markdown 3.11 (default and filter_tables/filter_code) gave identical output. - `pipecat.utils.tracing.service_decorators`: Exports tracedTts, tracedStt, tracedLlm, tracedGeminiLive(op), tracedOpenaiRealtime(op), plus installTracedTts/installTracedStt (the equivalent of upstream's __set_name__, for Node, which strips types and runs no decorator syntax) and the _get*/_add* helpers. DIVERGENCE: upstream checks tracing availability at import time; here the wrappers call isTracingAvailable() each time they run, because registerOpenTelemetry() happens at runtime. The decorators work as TC39 decorators (class-level patches go on each instance via addInitializer), as legacy experimentalDecorators, or as plain calls. start_as_current_span is emulated with startSpan plus api.context.with when the API provides it; exceptions are recorded with recordException/setStatus(ERROR) if the span has them. Span start/end times are passed as HrTime. Temporary pushFrame/startLlmUsageMetrics overrides in tracedLlm are restored by deleting the own property. Gemini Live messages are read with camelCase protocol fields (toolCall.functionCalls, usageMetadata.*TokenCount, serverContent.turnComplete). OpenAI Realtime events keep snake_case wire fields; _sessionProperties uses modelDump() if present, otherwise a shallow copy. JSON.stringify output uses compact separators, unlike Python json.dumps. Depends on unported service methods by convention name: TTS createAudioContext/appendToAudioContext/removeAudioContext/onAudioContextCompleted/resetActiveAudioContext/_audioContextManager.playingContextId; STT vadEnabled/_lastTranscriptTime. | UNRESOLVED: packages/core/src/workers/base_ui_worker.ts does not exist. - `pipecat.utils.tracing.setup`: DIVERGENCE: core cannot depend on OpenTelemetry, so the OTel JS modules are injected once with registerOpenTelemetry({ api, sdk, resources }), replacing upstream's try-import. isTracingAvailable() is true once they are registered. setupTracing({ serviceName, exporter, consoleExport }) uses the sdk-trace-base v2 API (BasicTracerProvider with spanProcessors, resourceFromAttributes, trace.setGlobalTracerProvider). The OTel types are structural interfaces (OtelSpan, OtelSpanContext, OtelContext, OtelTracer). Upstream's print() of setup errors is logger.error. | UNRESOLVED: packages/core/src/utils/security/redaction.ts: JS `\w` and `\b` only match ASCII, while Python's match Unicode. - `pipecat.workers`: Empty upstream __init__.py; index.ts is `export {}`. | UNRESOLVED: packages/core/src/bus/serializers/json.ts imports `@pipedog/bus-adapters/index` (LLMContextAdapter, ToolsSchemaAdapter), and that import does not resolve. @pipedog/core's package.json does not declare bus-adapters, bus-base, bus-llm-context-adapter or bus-tools-schema-adapter, and core's node_modules links only asyncio. Running the module fails with "Cannot find module '@pipedog/bus-adapters/index'". Those packages also depend on @pipedog/core, so declaring them would create a dependency cycle and break the core-takes-no-deps rule. The likely fix is to keep bus/adapters (base, llm_context_adapter, tools_schema_adapter) in core, but that is a manifest/packaging decision I was told not to make. I checked it with temporary symlinks, which I then removed: with the packages linked, serialize/deserialize round-trips and tsc reports no errors in the reviewed files. | UNRESOLVED: Minor, left as is: Python's json.dumps escapes non-ASCII and JSON.stringify does not, so the wire bytes differ but parse to the same values. JSONMessageSerializer serializes a dataclass from Object.entries, not dataclasses.fields, and passes every decoded field to the constructor instead of splitting init and non-init fields. str()/repr() of None, floats and bools in classifier error messages and in OpenAI Decisions probability keys does not match Python exactly (for example 'None' vs '', 1.0 vs 1).

- `pipecat.workers.base_worker`: Not ported (deprecated): the per-argument job spelling (name, payload, timeout, cancel_on_error on requestJob, job, requestJobGroup, jobGroup, createJobGroupAndRequestJob) and resolve_job_params. These methods take { params } only. BaseWorker extends BusSubscriber(BaseObject) (mixin). Methods that take *names plus keyword arguments take rest arguments and an optional trailing options object: addWorkers(a, b, { watch }), requestJobGroup('w1', 'w2', { params }), jobGroup(...). A non-string worker name throws TypeError. _waitWorkersReady returns a function to race with waitFor rather than a Future, because awaiting an async function that returns a Task would wait for the Task. A stable bound handler (_onWatchedWorkerReadyHandler) keeps registry watches idempotent, as Python's bound-method equality does. activeJobs and jobGroups are Maps; JobGroup.responses is a Record. WorkerActivationArgs.fromDict/toDict work from the instance's own fields. Cancelling a job handler cancels its Task, so an await on something other than an asyncio primitive inside a user handler stops only if it is passed the task's signal or wrapped in abortable(). | UNRESOLVED: packages/core/src/utils/security/redaction.ts: JS `\w` and `\b` only match ASCII, while Python's match Unicode. - `pipecat.workers.llm.backend_llm_worker`: DIVERGENCE: upstream overrides PipelineWorker.capabilities with a plain dict, and TS does not allow an override of a different type, so it is exposed as `backendCapabilities` (the attach update still sends it under the `capabilities` key). The @job handlers attach_frontend/handle_message are attachFrontend/handleMessage, registered in a static block. _BackendSession's async with becomes enter()/exit()/run(fn), and its async for becomes for-await with next(). In _ask, if the job fails before it starts, the response falls back to null (upstream would raise UnboundLocalError there). Calls llm.cancelFunctionCalls({reason}), which now exists in llm_service.ts. Payloads keep their snake_case wire keys. | UNRESOLVED: Minor truthiness edge cases left as they are, judged not worth the churn: UIWorker._screenJob uses `value ? LLMWorker registers tools through `registerFunction` with a bound wrapper rather than `_registerDirectFunction`. - `pipecat.workers.llm.llm_worker`: Dropped as deprecated: the messages/result_callback params of end() and activate_worker(), and _finish_function_call (used only by those params). DIVERGENCE: core has no context variables, so the _IN_TOOL_CALL ContextVar is a per-task WeakMap keyed by currentTask(). Tasks a tool handler spawns (gather/createTask) do not inherit it, so frames they queue are not deferred. Tools are registered with the public llm.registerFunction plus a bound DirectFunctionWrapper subclass instead of the private _register_direct_function, because a bound or wrapping function loses the source its parameter schema is parsed from. It is still an explicit registration, so advertised tools never auto-register over it. PipelineWorker accepts `active` in TS, so it is passed through options. | UNRESOLVED: Minor truthiness edge cases left as they are, judged not worth the churn: UIWorker._screenJob uses `value ? - `pipecat.workers.runner`: Ported to /Users/pol/vscode/pipedog/packages/core/src/workers/runner.ts. Dropped deprecated APIs: the `loop` constructor option and the `run(worker)` positional parameter. `run()` now takes `{ autoEnd }`. Divergences: (1) SIGINT/SIGTERM handlers are attached with `globalThis.process.on` only when the runtime has it (Bun, Node, Deno), and do nothing on Workers. (2) `_gcCollect` calls the global `gc()` only if the runtime exposes one (e.g. `--expose-gc`). It cannot log a collected count or `gc.garbage`. (3) The default name uses `crypto.randomUUID()`. The two `except asyncio.CancelledError: pass` blocks catch only CancelledError and rethrow anything else. The runSetupHook name stays `setup_worker_runner`, as upstream; the startup port also accepts the camelCase name. Remaining tsc errors are not in this file: `../pipeline/worker.ts` (PipelineWorker) and `./base_worker.ts` (BaseWorker, WorkerParams) are not written yet, and a TS6305 error says the `@pipedog/asyncio` dist build is stale. The port expects `BaseWorker.attach({ registry, bus, workerRunner })`, `worker.run(new WorkerParams({ taskManager }))`, the `parent`, `active`, `bridged` and `startedAt` getters, and `PipelineWorker.stopWhenDone()`. | UNRESOLVED: None in runner.ts. The rest of the port matches upstream: every method and branch, the on_ready and on_error event registrations, defaults (`||` is kept only where Python's `or` should also skip an empty string), cancellation (CancelledError is swallowed in run() and _runWorker as upstream does, and there are no other broad catches), gather with return_exceptions mapped to gatherSettled, and the deprecated `loop` parameter and `run(worker)` left out. Signals and garbage collection behave differently from Python because JavaScript has no equivalent, and both are marked DIVERGENCE in the code. | UNRESOLVED: Typecheck can't fully pass yet. ../pipeline/worker.ts and ./base_worker.ts are not ported, and the @pipedog/asyncio dist build is stale. This is expected while other modules are ported at the same time. | UNRESOLVED: The relayed user note says Effect could help with stopping work when a user interrupts. That is a decision about the core's architecture and was out of scope for this review. In runner.ts, cancellation already goes through @pipedog/asyncio Tasks (createTask, gatherSettled, Event.wait), so the note asks for no change here.

The only tsc error left is the missing ./ui_worker.ts, which another agent is porting. | UNRESOLVED: packages/core/src/workers/base_ui_worker.ts does not exist.
